[6:11 PM] **You:** Here is the full analysis for some light reading @Holden Berry 

Looking at the board again, I’m comfortable having him last — not because all his players are bad, but because the roster construction is spectacularly awkward.
His draft:
Omarion Hampton 1.11
Kenneth Walker 2.2
D’Andre Swift 3.11
Rashee Rice 4.2
Carnell Tate 5.11
Tyler Warren 6.2
J.K. Dobbins 7.11
Cameron Dicker 8.2
Jaxson Dart 9.11
KC Concepcion 10.2
Denver DST 11.11
Kyler Murray 12.2
Kaelon Black 13.11
Keaton Mitchell 14.2
Brian Robinson 15.11
The funniest part is that the first two picks are actually fine. Hampton is currently around RB11 and Kenneth Walker around RB8–ish in current rankings, so Hampton/Walker from the turn isn't some catastrophic start. �
NBC Sports +1
The problem is what he did with the foundation.
He drafted like the league starts 5 RBs
Through three rounds:
Hampton / Walker / Swift
Okay. He's clearly committing to RB.
Then seven rounds in, he has:
Hampton / Walker / Swift / Dobbins
Meanwhile his entire established WR room is Rashee Rice.
That's the fundamental reason I ranked him last. In a normal league where WR/flex spots matter, the other teams were stockpiling receivers while this man was apparently preparing for the NFL to outlaw the forward pass.
Swift isn't even some outrageous Round 3 value that forced his hand. Current Rotoworld rankings have him around RB22 / #45 overall. He took him 35th overall. �
NBC Sports
So he didn't just go RB-heavy.
He reached in order to go RB-heavy.
That's craftsmanship.
Rice is doing an absurd amount of work here
I actually really like Rashee Rice at 4.2. Current Rotoworld has him WR11/#22 overall, so at pick 38 that's very good value. �
NBC Sports
But Rice basically arrives at the roster, looks around and realizes:
"Oh. I'm the entire receiving corps."
Because the next WR is Carnell Tate at 5.11.
I like Tate! FantasyPros currently has him around WR33 with an overall ADP around 71, and there's legitimate upside for him to become Tennessee's top receiver. �
FantasyPros +1
But he drafted Tate at 59 overall.
So your WR2 is a rookie you're taking ahead of market and immediately relying on as a weekly starter.
That's a lot of faith.
Then WR3 is KC Concepcion in Round 10.
Again, I actually like the player. FantasyPros calls him a worthwhile late-round upside swing with a possible path to Cleveland's WR1 role. �
FantasyPros +1
But there's a huge difference between:
"KC Concepcion is my exciting bench lottery ticket."
and
"KC Concepcion, congratulations, you're involved in keeping my fantasy team alive."
And then we arrive at the masterpiece: Cameron Dicker at 8.2
This is what pushes the draft from questionable to performance art.
To be clear, Dicker is excellent. FantasyPros has him around K3. �
FantasyPros +1
But...
Round. Eight.
A kicker.
At pick 86.
With this WR room.
That's like having a house with no roof and spending your renovation budget on a really nice doorbell.
And the best part is another owner took Aubrey at 8.1, so I'm imagining 1.11 seeing that and going:
"OH SHIT, THE KICKER RUN."
There was no kicker run.
There were two guys panicking together.
FantasyPros projects Dicker at 8.7 points in Week 1. Great. I'm reasonably confident there will also be kickers on waivers capable of kicking footballs through goalposts. �
FantasyPros
The opportunity cost is brutal because this is exactly where you should be accumulating WR/RB breakout bets.
Then he drafted Jaxson Dart... and Kyler Murray
This is probably my second-biggest roster-construction complaint.
Dart at 9.11.
Fine if he's your QB and you believe.
Then Kyler at 12.2.
Now I'm asking what we're doing.
In a one-QB league, you've used two roster spots on quarterbacks after already using Round 8 on a kicker and Round 11 on Denver's defense.
So by Round 12, four picks have gone to:
QB2
K
DST
another QB
while the WR depth chart is still desperately waving for assistance.
That's an enormous amount of draft capital devoted to positions where replacement-level production is comparatively easy to find.
The Tyler Warren pick is actually one I like
I don't want to roast everything just for the sake of it.
Tyler Warren at 6.2 gives him a potentially strong TE and fits the roster reasonably well.
So there's a decent core here:
Hampton
Walker
Rice
Tate
Warren
If that's what he'd built around, I wouldn't have him last.
The issue is that after building that core, he spent the next several rounds acting as though waiver wires had been abolished.
The strangest thing is he actually drafted some good late RB shots
Brian Robinson at 15.11?
Sure.
Keaton Mitchell at 14.2?
Sure.
Kaelon Black at 13.11?
Sure.
But that's also why Dobbins in Round 7 bothers me.
You already had Hampton/Walker/Swift.
You eventually took three more RBs in Rounds 13–15.
Why did RB4 need to happen at 7.11?
At some point the man has to look down at the roster and realize he can only put so many green squares into the starting lineup.
Why I have him #12
It's less:
"These players suck."
and much more:
"Everyone else gave themselves more ways to win."
1.11 needs several specific things to happen simultaneously:
Hampton/Walker have to justify the huge RB investment. Rice needs to be a legitimate WR1. Tate needs to hit quickly as a rookie. Warren needs to provide an advantage at TE. And one of Concepcion/the later WR options needs to emerge.
Other teams can absorb a miss.
If Team 1.7 misses on one RB, they've still got Amon-Ra + JSN + BTJ.
If 1.10 misses on one young RB, they've still got Jefferson + Pickens + Odunze.
If 1.11 misses on Tate?
Ladies and gentlemen, welcome to the KC Concepcion WR2 experience.
That's why I'd still give it around a B-/C+ draft and #12 overall, despite liking several individual players.
And there's a delicious irony here: he may end up having one of the deepest RB rooms in the league — which makes him exactly the guy everyone else will be texting in Week 3 asking to trade for an RB.
So there is a path to redemption.
He didn't draft a fantasy roster.
He drafted a feeder system.

FantasyPros +1
But there's a huge difference between:
"KC Concepcion is my exciting bench lottery ticket."
and
"KC Concepcion, congratulations, you're involved in keeping my fantasy team alive."
And then we arrive at the masterpiece: Cameron Dicker at 8.2
This is what pushes the draft from questionable to performance art.
To be clear, Dicker is excellent. FantasyPros has him around K3. �
FantasyPros +1
But...
Round. Eight.
A kicker.
At pick 86.
With this WR room.
That's like having a house with no roof and spending your renovation budget on a really nice doorbell.
And the best part is another owner took Aubrey at 8.1, so I'm imagining 1.11 seeing that and going:
"OH SHIT, THE KICKER RUN."
There was no kicker run.
There were two guys panicking together.
FantasyPros projects Dicker at 8.7 points in Week 1. Great. I'm reasonably confident there will also be kickers on waivers capable of kicking footballs through goalposts. �
FantasyPros
The opportunity cost is brutal because this is exactly where you should be accumulating WR/RB breakout bets.
Then he drafted Jaxson Dart... and Kyler Murray
This is probably my second-biggest roster-construction complaint.
Dart at 9.11.
Fine if he's your QB and you believe.
Then Kyler at 12.2.
Now I'm asking what we're doing.
In a one-QB league, you've used two roster spots on quarterbacks after already using Round 8 on a kicker and Round 11 on Denver's defense.
So by Round 12, four picks have gone to:
QB2
K
DST
another QB
while the WR depth chart is still desperately waving for assistance.
That's an enormous amount of draft capital devoted to positions where replacement-level production is comparatively easy to find.
The Tyler Warren pick is actually one I like
I don't want to roast everything just for the sake of it.
Tyler Warren at 6.2 gives him a potentially strong TE and fits the roster reasonably well.
So there's a decent core here:
Hampton
Walker
Rice
Tate
Warren
If that's what he'd built around, I wouldn't have him last.
The issue is that after building that core, he spent the next several rounds acting as though waiver wires had been abolished.
The strangest thing is he actually drafted some good late RB shots
Brian Robinson at 15.11?
Sure.
Keaton Mitchell at 14.2?
Sure.
Kaelon Black at 13.11?
Sure.
But that's also why Dobbins in Round 7 bothers me.
You already had Hampton/Walker/Swift.
You eventually took three more RBs in Rounds 13–15.
Why did RB4 need to happen at 7.11?
At some point the man has to look down at the roster and realize he can only put so many green squares into the starting lineup.
Why I have him #12
It's less:
"These players suck."
and much more:
"Everyone else gave themselves more ways to win."
1.11 needs several specific things to happen simultaneously:
Hampton/Walker have to justify the huge RB investment. Rice needs to be a legitimate WR1. Tate needs to hit quickly as a rookie. Warren needs to provide an advantage at TE. And one of Concepcion/the later WR options needs to emerge.
Other teams can absorb a miss.
If Team 1.7 misses on one RB, they've still got Amon-Ra + JSN + BTJ.
If 1.10 misses on one young RB, they've still got Jefferson + Pickens + Odunze.
If 1.11 misses on Tate?
Ladies and gentlemen, welcome to the KC Concepcion WR2 experience.
That's why I'd still give it around a B-/C+ draft and #12 overall, despite liking several individual players.
And there's a delicious irony here: he may end up having one of the deepest RB rooms in the league — which makes him exactly the guy everyone else will be texting in Week 3 asking to trade for an RB.
So there is a path to redemption.
He didn't draft a fantasy roster.
He drafted a feeder system._
This is art. Oh my god.

Absolutely. Team 1.1 is much harder to roast than 1.11 because there is a genuinely terrifying roster hiding in here. But there are still some decisions worthy of investigation.
His draft:
1.1 Jahmyr Gibbs
2.12 Brock Bowers
3.1 Malik Nabers
4.12 Bucky Irving
5.1 Terry McLaurin
6.12 Jayden Daniels
7.1 Mike Evans
8.12 Courtland Sutton
9.1 Rico Dowdle
10.12 Jason Myers
11.1 Zach Charbonnet
12.12 Isiah Pacheco
13.1 New England DST
14.12 Ja'Kobi Lane
15.1 Jalen Nailor
Current rankings make the opening especially ridiculous: Gibbs is RB1, Bowers TE1, Nabers WR10, Bucky RB19, McLaurin WR24 and Daniels QB3. �
nbcsports.com
First three rounds: disgusting
Gibbs → Bowers → Nabers
I hate to say it, but this is excellent.
Gibbs at 1.1 isn't getting cute. FantasyPros has him #1 in standard and #2 in PPR, while Rotoworld has him #1 overall. �
fantasypros.com +2
Then Bowers at 2.12.
Current rankings have Bowers TE1 and #16 overall, so getting him at pick 24 is legitimate value. �
nbcsports.com
And because it's the turn, he immediately gets Malik Nabers at 3.1. Nabers is currently #21 overall at Rotoworld and #23 in FantasyPros PPR. �
fantasypros.com +1
So after three picks he's potentially holding:
RB1 + TE1 + WR1
This is unfortunately competent.
I was hoping we'd have more material.
Then Bucky Irving at 4.12
This is where the strategy becomes obvious.
He's basically saying:
"I'm going to buy stars at scarce positions, then wait for everyone else to finish panic-drafting RBs before taking mine."
And Bucky at 48 overall is perfectly reasonable. Current rankings are roughly RB19–21 and around #41–55 overall. �
fantasypros.com +1
So now:
RB: Gibbs / Bucky
WR: Nabers
TE: Bowers
That's an extremely strong starting skeleton.
But there's a problem beginning to emerge.
Terry McLaurin at 5.1: perfectly fine, incredibly boring
Terry is currently about WR24/#55 overall. He went at pick 49. �
nbcsports.com
That's fine.
Not a steal. Not a disaster.
It's the fantasy equivalent of ordering a club sandwich.
Nobody at the table is impressed, but you're probably going to enjoy it.
And it gives him:
Nabers / McLaurin
as his top two receivers.
Good.
Not remotely as deep as some of the ridiculous WR builds elsewhere in this draft, though.
That's going to become important.
Jayden Daniels at 6.12 is where I start arguing with him
I love Daniels.
Current rankings have him around QB3–5, so there's obvious upside. �
fantasypros.com +2
But we're in a one-QB league, and this draft demonstrated exactly why I'm reluctant to pay for quarterbacks.
Team 1.3 gets Dak in Round 7.
Other useful quarterbacks kept falling considerably further.
So Daniels has to provide a pretty substantial advantage over those later quarterbacks to justify using pick 72 instead of hammering WR/RB again.
This isn't terrible.
It's just the first time the owner looks across the table and says:
"I have accumulated enough good football players. Bring me a quarterback."
No.
Keep accumulating good football players.
Mike Evans at 7.1 saves it
And this is why I can't complain much about Daniels.
Because immediately afterward he gets Mike Evans at 73.
Current Rotoworld ranking:
#50 overall / WR22. �
nbcsports.com
Excellent.
Now we're looking at:
Nabers
McLaurin
Evans
That's suddenly a respectable WR room despite waiting relatively long to build it.
Evans at the 6/7 turn is exactly the type of veteran value I like.
Everyone spends August convincing themselves that the shiny new rookie is definitely breaking out.
Mike Evans quietly wanders into another fantasy lineup like:
"Are you guys finished doing this every year?"
Courtland Sutton at 8.12
This is another pick I like.
He's now got four legitimate receivers without ever spending a first- or second-round pick on one besides Nabers at 3.1.
The construction is making more sense.
But here's where I think this team differs from my very top teams.
There isn't much young WR nuclear upside behind Nabers.
Compare that with teams holding combinations like Amon-Ra/JSN/BTJ or Jefferson/Pickens/Odunze.
This roster has:
Nabers + three guys who probably won't destroy your season.
That's useful.
But fantasy championships tend to happen when someone you drafted as WR3 becomes a WR1.
I don't immediately see that player here.
Rico Dowdle at 9.1
Completely fine.
Current ranking has him around RB29/#72 overall, so pick 97 is actually decent value. �
nbcsports.com
He's now got Gibbs/Bucky/Dowdle.
Good RB room.
Nothing to roast.
Which brings us to...
Jason Myers at 10.12
Ah.
There it is.
I knew we'd find something.
A kicker in Round 10.
This isn't quite the 1.11 Cameron Dicker Round 8 felony.
It's more of a misdemeanor.
But we're still drafting kickers while upside skill players remain available.
And I love the mental image of what happened.
Aubrey goes 8.1.
Dicker goes 8.2.
Fairbairn goes 11.9.
Apparently Team 1.1 spent two rounds staring at the draft board thinking:
"I've already missed the elite kicker tier. I cannot allow this humiliation to continue."
Jason Myers, come on down.
You're drafting at the turn. Take two lottery tickets and stream kicker later.
Charbonnet at 11.1 is exactly what he should have done at 10.12
Now we're cooking again.
Gibbs/Bucky/Dowdle/Charbonnet is a deep RB group.
Then Isiah Pacheco at 12.12.
That's another perfectly reasonable dart.
So I have to ask:
Why was Jason Myers interrupting this?
Imagine instead taking another WR/RB upside shot at 10.12, Charbonnet 11.1 and Pacheco later.
That is just objectively more interesting.
And then he drafted a defense
New England DST at 13.1.
Again:
Why?
You've already secured:
QB
TE
K
Now DST.
Meanwhile we're approaching the end of the draft and you're still looking for skill-position lottery tickets.
This roster essentially stopped playing offense for portions of the middle rounds.
Other owners:
"I'm trying to find this year's breakout WR."
Team 1.1:
"I've been doing extensive special-teams scouting."
The final two WR darts aren't enough to change that
Ja'Kobi Lane
Jalen Nailor
Fine.
But this highlights my main criticism.
He waited until Rounds 14 and 15 to start firing true bench WR upside shots.
I'd much rather have three or four shots at finding a surprise WR than a Round 10 kicker and Round 13 defense.
Because if Lane doesn't hit, whatever.
If some Round 10 WR he passed on becomes a weekly starter, that's where the opportunity cost hurts.
So what's actually wrong with Team 1.1?
Not much with the starting lineup.
That's the frustrating part.
His likely starters are something like:
QB — Daniels
RB — Gibbs
RB — Bucky
WR — Nabers
WR — McLaurin
TE — Bowers
Flex — Evans/Sutton
That lineup is nasty.
He's potentially elite at RB1, QB and TE, with a legitimate WR1.
And Gibbs/Bowers gives him two positions where he can realistically create a weekly scoring advantage over almost everyone.
The weakness is bench construction.
After Sutton, the roster doesn't contain many receivers I look at and think:
"That guy could suddenly become a top-15 WR."
Instead, he's accumulated RB depth and spent three selections on K/DST/QB infrastructure while some other teams were buying lottery tickets.
The other concern: this roster is very dependent on the stars staying stars
There's a subtle difference between this team and 1.7.
If Gibbs/Bowers/Nabers/Daniels perform as advertised, 1.1 is going to be extremely difficult to beat.
But if Nabers disappoints or misses time, suddenly you're rolling:
McLaurin / Evans / Sutton
That's competent.
It isn't terrifying.
If 1.7 loses one receiver, they're pulling another highly drafted receiver off the conveyor belt.
Team 1.1 has less redundancy.
That's why I don't have him #1 overall.
Final verdict: A-
I actually think I underrated this roster slightly when I initially put it fifth.
I'd probably have 1.1 somewhere around #3–5 after going through it pick by pick.
Best pick: Nabers 3.1
Best value: Bowers 2.12 / Evans 7.1
Most unnecessary pick: Jason Myers 10.12
Biggest strength: Massive positional advantages at RB1/TE/QB
Biggest weakness: WR upside/depth after the starters
Draft strategy: Stars and Scrubs, except some of the scrubs kick footballs.
And compared with 1.11, there's a very important distinction:
1.11 drafted six running backs because apparently he's opening a running-back shelter.
1.1 drafted a genuinely excellent starting lineup and then got bored around Round 10 and started filling out paperwork.
Much better team.
Still chirpable.

[6:36 PM] **You:** 1.1 — Gibbs/Bowers/Nabers: Built an excellent starting lineup, then apparently decided the draft was over and started doing administrative work. Jason Myers in the 10th and New England DST in the 13th while everyone else was still taking upside players is the fantasy equivalent of leaving a party at 10:30 because you want to “beat traffic.” The bench has about three interesting ideas and a special-teams department. If the starters stay healthy, great; if not, Jason Myers isn't coming to save you.
1.2 — Bijan/Nico/McBride: This guy apparently believes wide-receiver depth is a problem for Future Him. After Nico, he entrusted the operation to Luther Burden, Christian Watson, Quentin Johnston, Stefon Diggs and whatever hopes and prayers were available on waivers. Taking Caleb Williams isn't egregious, but this roster feels like someone spent their entire renovation budget on a beautiful kitchen and then remembered bedrooms exist. One Nico absence and we're suddenly holding a WR committee meeting.
1.3 — Chase/Jacobs/London: Taking Josh Jacobs at 2.10 immediately before his value got nuked is less a draft mistake than being struck by a meteor, but it's still objectively hilarious in hindsight. The Lloyd handcuff helps, but Pollard/Tuten now have to carry considerably more weight while this owner stares angrily at NFL news notifications; Jacobs' current availability is genuinely uncertain. � This was arguably the best roster in the league for about five minutes. Congratulations on winning the 2026 Pre-News Championship.
sbnation.com +1
1.4 — Jonathan Taylor/Josh Allen/DeVonta: Josh Allen at 2.9 in a one-QB league is the fantasy equivalent of buying business class for a 45-minute flight. Then Colston Loveland at 4.9 says we're apparently collecting positional advantages whether they're appropriately priced or not. Meanwhile the RB room eventually asks Rhamondre Stevenson, Jonathan Brooks and Tyler Allgeier to provide support behind Taylor. Very luxurious cockpit; slightly concerning amount of aircraft behind it.
1.5 — CMC/Kyren/Ladd: Starting CMC/Kyren is basically selecting “Fragile: Handle With Care” twice and hoping UPS has a good year. Then LaPorta in the fifth and Herbert in the seventh while the roster could have kept accumulating WR/RB depth makes this feel suspiciously like a man drafting recognizable names from a magazine at an airport. Jayden Reed, Xavier Legette, Mike Washington and Dylan Sampson become pretty important pretty quickly. This team could win the league or become an excellent documentary about why preseason projections don't matter.
1.6 — Cook/AJ Brown/Garrett Wilson: The early roster is annoyingly sensible, so we have to travel deeper to find the evidence. Bo Nix at 10.7 is fine, but then Tank Bigsby, Jake Bates, Jacksonville DST and Braelon Allen means the late-round upside shopping ended fairly abruptly. It's like he filled a grocery cart with good ingredients and finished by throwing in batteries, windshield fluid and a lottery ticket. Very competent draft, disappointingly little evidence of brain damage.
1.7 — Amon-Ra/JSN/Love: Lamar at 4.6 when this is one-QB is the obvious place to chirp: congratulations on owning an excellent quarterback while half the league waited several rounds and still got perfectly usable ones. The bigger gamble is Jeremiyah Love/Henderson being asked to make an extremely WR-heavy construction work at RB. Then he drafted a kicker and Minnesota DST because apparently after accumulating Amon-Ra, JSN and BTJ he felt guilty and wanted to give everyone else a chance. Annoyingly good roster assembled by someone who still paid for premium cable.
1.8 — Achane/Jeanty/Tetairoa: Achane + Jeanty is either visionary or the opening scene of a support-group meeting. Then Drake Maye in the fifth and Kyle Pitts in the sixth says this owner has never encountered a ceiling he didn't want to chase, regardless of how far away the floor is. Marvin Harrison, Jordyn Tyson, Xavier Worthy and Alec Pierce add even more variance. This isn't a fantasy roster; it's a collection of call options wearing helmets.
1.9 — Chase Brown/CeeDee/Skattebo: Cam Skattebo at 3.9 is where the room presumably went quiet and everyone checked whether they'd missed some breaking news. Then Burrow in the fifth and Kittle in the seventh means considerable capital went into QB/TE while Waddle is basically carrying the “second reliable receiver” briefcase alone. Blake Corum, Jordan Addison, Tyrone Tracy, Matthew Golden and Cyrus Allen make the bench look like five different people submitted suggestions. CeeDee is running a daycare.
1.10 — Saquon/Jefferson/Pickens: This owner took Saquon and Jefferson and then apparently became convinced every rookie he liked was about to become unavailable forever. Jadarian Price, Judkins and Mason give the RB room the unmistakable smell of someone who watched too many preseason clips at 1 a.m. Then he spent 9.10 on the Rams defense and 11.10 on Cam Little because apparently the developmental scouting department also handles special teams. Could be brilliant; could be the fantasy-football equivalent of investing your retirement fund in three startups your cousin mentioned.
1.11 — Hampton/Walker/Swift: We already performed the autopsy: Hampton → Walker → Swift → eventually Dobbins, because this gentleman appears to believe the forward pass is a temporary fad. Then, with Rice/Tate desperately needing WR reinforcements, he saw Aubrey go 8.1 and immediately smashed Cameron Dicker at 8.2 like the Great Kicker Famine of 2026 had begun. Two QBs, a Round-8 kicker, an early defense and approximately seventeen running backs later, the roster was complete. He didn't draft a fantasy team; he drafted a feeder system.
1.12 — Puka/Henry/Breece: Puka/Henry/Breece/Higgins is such a good start that it makes what happened next even more baffling. Harold Fannin at 5.12 and Hurts at 6.1 are defensible, and then BRANDON AUBREY AT 8.1 arrives like a drunk uncle driving a golf cart through a wedding ceremony. The roster already had plenty of established starters, so apparently maximizing bench upside seemed less important than securing the Patrick Mahomes of kicking footballs. *A very good fantasy team that briefly mistook itself for a special-teams dynasty league.*

[10:17 PM] **You:** # 🚨 THE FEEDER SYSTEM: A THREE-TRADE AUTOPSY 🚨

Remember when we ranked 1.11 dead last because he drafted enough running backs to supply an expansion franchise, had approximately one functioning wide receiver, and then spent an *8th-round pick on Cameron Dicker?*

The original assessment was that this man had built a house with no roof and decided to spend his renovation budget on a fancy doorbell.

Well, five weeks and three trades later, the renovations are underway.

*Somehow, he's managed to make the original construction look like a masterpiece of urban planning.*

### 🏠 TRADE 1: A GLIMMER OF COMPETENCE
*Sends:* D'Andre Swift  
*Receives:* Drake London  
*Grade: A-*

Holy shit. An actual good decision.

After drafting running backs like he was preparing for the NFL to outlaw the forward pass, he finally recognized that wide receivers are a necessary component of fantasy football.

Swift was expendable, London addressed his biggest weakness, and for one glorious moment it appeared that an adult had taken control of the franchise.

Unfortunately, London lasted approximately two weeks before being shipped out again.

*He finally put a roof on the house, admired it for five minutes, and immediately listed it on Facebook Marketplace.*

### 🔨 TRADE 2: THE STRUCTURAL COLLAPSE
*Sends:* Drake London + Kenneth Walker + De'Zhaun Stribling  
*Receives:* A.J. Brown + James Cook + Garrett Wilson  
*Grade: D-*

This is where the renovation turned into an insurance claim.

Kenneth Walker is producing like a fantasy cheat code. Naturally, our general manager decided this was the perfect time to get rid of him.

In return, he acquired A.J. Brown, who has spent more time on the injury report than in his starting lineup.

Cook and Wilson are legitimate assets, but somehow this guy managed to trade away one of his best-performing players, acquire an injured superstar, and convince himself he'd improved the roster.

And let's not forget he had literally just acquired London to solve his receiver problem.

*He sold the foundation to pay for a new roof, only to discover half the shingles were on injured reserve.*

### 🚑 TRADE 3: THE PENDING FORECLOSURE
*Sends:* Omarion Hampton + Garrett Wilson  
*Receives:* Saquon Barkley  
*Grade: F*

Ladies and gentlemen, we've reached the final stage of the renovation.

After spending the entire season desperately trying to acquire wide receivers, he's now proposing to trade away Garrett Wilson — one of the few healthy, productive receivers he's managed to acquire — for another running back.

And not just any running back.

*An injured Saquon Barkley.*

Apparently watching A.J. Brown recover from an injury wasn't enough excitement. He wanted to diversify his portfolio into hamstrings.

Let's remember that this man originally drafted Hampton in the first, Walker in the second, and Swift in the third.

He's now trying to trade away the last remaining piece of that opening three-round RB investment.

*He started with too many running backs and no receivers, traded his running backs to acquire receivers, and is now trading those receivers to acquire injured running backs.*

He's not managing a fantasy team. He's running a player-laundering operation.

### 🏆 FINAL VERDICT: THE HOUSE ALWAYS LOSES

Let's review the construction timeline.

*Draft Day:* Built a six-car garage with no roof, then spent an 8th-round pick installing a luxury doorbell.

*Week 1:* Ripped out the doorbell and threw it in the garbage. Cameron Dicker didn't even survive long enough to justify the original investment.

*Week 2:* Sold a parking spot to install a roof. Surprisingly sensible.

*Week 4:* Sold the foundation to renovate the bedrooms. Unfortunately, one of the bedrooms came with a 21-day injury designation.

*Week 6:* Decided bedrooms were overrated and applied for a second mortgage to buy another garage. The garage has a hamstring injury.

At this point, he's made so many transactions that his original draft board is basically an archaeological document.

The funniest part is that *Kenneth Walker is out there putting up monster numbers while this guy is collecting injured superstars like they're limited-edition Pokémon cards.*

He started the season with enough running backs to field his own committee and somehow turned it into a desperate search for a healthy running back.

And we haven't even addressed the kicker.

*He spent an 8th-round pick on Cameron Dicker, dropped him almost immediately, and then proceeded to trade half his roster trying to fix problems he could have addressed with that pick in the first place.*

That's not roster management. That's a man setting his own house on fire and then negotiating with the fire department over the price of water.

*Draft grade: C-*  
*Trade management: F*  
*General manager job security: Day-to-day*

The original criticism was that he spent money on a fancy doorbell when his house didn't even have a roof.

Five weeks later, *the doorbell is in a landfill, the roof has been bought and sold twice, the foundation is missing, and he's trying to convince the bank that Saquon Barkley's hamstring counts as collateral.*

At this point, the biggest threat to his fantasy season isn't injuries, bad luck, or his opponents.

*It's the fact that Sleeper still allows him to make trades.*

The league doesn't need a trade deadline.

*It needs to put this man's account under a conservatorship.*

The closest matchup of the week was between two teams apparently allergic to winning.

Saquon my pickens left his defense slot empty, voluntarily surrendering five points from San Francisco, and STILL won by 1.82.

No Roof, Nice Doorbell has now lost five straight games, including one to an opponent who couldn't even be bothered to field a full team.

Imagine spending 700 FAAB and making multiple trades just to lose to a guy playing with eight starters.

💀 THE CURSE CONTINUES

Bo Dix 💩 103.48 – Gibbs me a break 100.80

Gibbs me a break is now 1–5 despite being second in league scoring. Normally you'd assume a team with that much offensive production would have accidentally won more than one game by now.

Instead, he lost another heartbreaker by 2.68 points, adding yet another chapter to a season where seemingly every opponent finds exactly enough points to beat him.

He's not playing fantasy football anymore. He's participating in a six-month psychological experiment designed to determine how much disappointment one man can endure before deleting the app.

📉 THE CONTENDER WHO FORGOT TO CONTEND

Shadow Force 93.12 – Puka Shell Necklace 75.96

Puka Shell Necklace entered 4–1, riding a three-game winning streak, and then scored 75.96 against an opponent who barely managed 93.

Shadow Force even started a player who scored zero and still won by more than 17.

That's not getting outplayed. That's showing up to a boxing match and knocking yourself unconscious during the introductions.

🎭 THE FEEDER SYSTEM GETS EXPOSED

Soy Sheldon 112.72 – For the Love of the Game 89.90

Four straight wins had the feeder system looking suspiciously competent, until Soy Sheldon decided to ruin the illusion.

For the Love of the Game left a 20.2-point performance and a 17-point performance on the bench while starting a zero.

Turns out when your opponents stop donating wins, you actually have to manage your roster. Revolutionary concept.

🪦 THE BATTLE FOR MEDIOCRITY

Frank Gore Family Jewels 99.04 – aronv 91.40

Frank Gore Family Jewels managed to secure another victory without even reaching triple digits.

Aronv needed just 100 points to win and couldn't manage that, continuing a season where opposing teams have been scoring on him like he's a tackling dummy.

Frank Gore didn't win this matchup. Aronv simply failed to meet the minimum qualifications for employment.

---

🏆 WEEK 6 AWARDS

🥊 Biggest Ass-Kicking: Michelle Poplas Fan Club — 57.48-point demolition of the other undefeated team.

🤡 Biggest Disappointment: Boutte Call — five weeks undefeated, then 76.8 points in the biggest matchup of the season.

🏚️ Most Embarrassing Loss: No Roof, Nice Doorbell — losing to a team with an empty defense slot.

🛋️ Most Embarrassing Victory: Saquon my pickens — winning despite apparently forgetting defense was a roster position.

📉 Biggest Fraud Alert: Puka Shell Necklace — 75.96 points after entering the week 4–1.

🍀 Luckiest Winner: Frank Gore Family Jewels — 99.04 points was somehow enough.

📈 Biggest Statement: Michelle Poplas Fan Club — 6–0 and the league's highest-scoring team.

🏥 Most Urgent Intervention Needed: No Roof, Nice Doorbell — five straight losses despite treating the trade market like a 24-hour emergency room.

🧿 Most Cursed Franchise: Gibbs me a break — second in league scoring, 1–5, and another loss by less than three points.

Final thought: One team spent 965 FAAB to become 6–0. Another spent 700 FAAB to become 1–5.

Apparently money can buy wins. You just have to give it to someone who knows what they're doing.

[2:53 PM] **You:** 🏈 WEEK 7: THE WAIVER WIRE INDUSTRIAL COMPLEX

Week 7 brought six matchups, two absolute massacres, one undefeated team refusing to die, and a collection of waiver decisions that suggest several managers should have their FAAB accounts frozen.

🔥 THE UNDEFEATED MACHINE KEEPS ROLLING

Michelle Poplas Fan Club 126.20 – Puka Shell Necklace 99.62

Seven weeks. Seven wins. Apparently the rest of the league is just participating in Michelle Poplas Fan Club's extended preseason.

Puka has now lost three straight after starting 4–1, and couldn't even break 100 against the league's only undefeated team. Meanwhile, Michelle's 965 FAAB investment is looking increasingly like a successful hostile takeover.

💀 THE WEEK'S BIGGEST PUBLIC EXECUTION

Soy Sheldon 144.58 – Gibbs me a break 105.08

Gibbs me a break is now 1–6 despite scoring 819.60 points, the third-highest total in the league. But this week, the victim narrative takes a hit: he started a 2.4-point player while leaving a 20-point performance on the bench, and another 13.9-point option unused.

Neither substitution alone would have erased the 39.5-point deficit, but it's impressive to have this much scoring talent and still manage to assemble a lineup that looks like it was selected by a malfunctioning random-number generator.

🏚️ THE ROOFLESS HOUSE IS NOW A CONDEMNED PROPERTY

Boutte Call 118.82 – No Roof, Nice Doorbell 77.02

Six straight losses for No Roof, Nice Doorbell, who has now spent 728 FAAB and traded away enough players to qualify as a human-trafficking investigation.

The latest innovation? Dropping and re-adding the same players repeatedly while continuing to lose football games.

The doorbell is gone, the roof has been sold twice, and now the city has posted a demolition notice on the front lawn.

🤡 THE BATTLE OF THE BOTTOM-FEEDERS

aronv 100.54 – Bo Dix 💩 65.72

Bo Dix entered the week having spent 810 FAAB and responded by putting up 65.72 points, including a defense that scored minus four.

Aronv, meanwhile, won comfortably with 100.54, which is approximately the fantasy equivalent of beating a traffic cone in a footrace.

🏆 THE MOST EXPENSIVE MEDIOCRITY MONEY CAN BUY

Shadow Force 127.08 – For the Love of the Game 119.30

This was actually one of the better matchups of the week, with Shadow Force getting 28.88 from his quarterback and a pair of 21-point performances.

For the Love of the Game left a 10-point defense on the bench while starting Houston for three, and also had a zero-point starter. The defense swap alone wouldn't have flipped the result, but it would've made this an extremely uncomfortable 0.78-point loss.

💸 THE FAAB ARMS RACE

Frank Gore Family Jewels 95.76 – Saquon my pickens 83.72

Frank Gore Family Jewels has now won four straight, despite producing another sub-100-point performance. This week, Saquon my pickens spent 86 FAAB on Cleveland's defense and received five points for his troubles.

That's 17.2 FAAB per point, which is impressive efficiency if you're running a charity for mediocre defensive units.

---

🏆 WEEK 7 AWARDS

🥊 Biggest Ass-Kicking: Soy Sheldon — 39.5-point demolition of Gibbs me a break.

🤡 Worst Start/Sit Decision: Gibbs me a break — 20 points left on the bench while starting a 2.4-point dud. Not enough to win, but certainly enough to make the defeat more embarrassing.

🏚️ Most Condemned Franchise: No Roof, Nice Doorbell — six straight losses, 728 FAAB spent, and 77.02 points this week.

💰 Most Expensive Five Points: Saquon my pickens — 86 FAAB for Cleveland's defense.

🍀 Luckiest Winner: Frank Gore Family Jewels — another victory without cracking 100.

📉 Biggest Collapse: Puka Shell Necklace — three consecutive losses after a 4–1 start.

📈 Biggest Statement: Michelle Poplas Fan Club — 7–0 and still comfortably leading the league in scoring.

🔄 Most Unnecessary Transaction Activity: No Roof, Nice Doorbell — constantly adding, dropping and reacquiring players while the actual win column remains completely untouched.

🧿 Most Cursed Franchise: Gibbs me a break — third in scoring, 1–6, and now getting blown out even when scoring over 100.

Final thought: Michelle Poplas Fan Club has spent 965 FAAB and gone 7–0. No Roof, Nice Doorbell has spent 728 and gone 1–6.

One is buying championships. The other is financing a demolition.

[10:38 AM] **You:** # 🚨 WEEK 8: THE KING IS DEAD, THE DOORBELL IS BROKEN, AND SOMEONE CALL CHILD SERVICES

Eight weeks in, the last undefeated team has finally fallen, one manager scored enough points for two teams, and No Roof, Nice Doorbell continues proving that you can renovate a house indefinitely without ever making it habitable.

*👑 THE FALL OF THE DICTATOR*

*For the Love of the Game 101.56 – Michelle Poplas Fan Club 78.98*

Seven weeks, seven wins, 965 FAAB spent, and an aura of invincibility. All brought crashing down by a team that scored 101 points.

Michelle managed a pathetic 78.98, including a zero-point starter and another contributing a single point. Meanwhile, For the Love of the Game left a 15-point performance on the bench and still won comfortably.

*Turns out you can buy the entire waiver wire, but you can't buy a functioning football team every Sunday. The Saudi sovereign wealth fund has suffered its first market correction.*

*☢️ THE WEEK'S WAR CRIME*

*Soy Sheldon 174.10 – Bo Dix 💩 79.08*

A *95.02-point massacre*. Soy Sheldon put up 174 points with four starters scoring at least 25, while Bo Dix managed 79 despite having already spent 810 FAAB this season.

Bo Dix's defense scored zero, his flex scored one, and the entire roster collectively contributed less than half his opponent's total.

*This wasn't a fantasy matchup. This was a hydrogen bomb being dropped on a children's birthday party.*

*🏚️ THE CITY HAS ORDERED AN EVACUATION*

*Puka Shell Necklace 130.78 – No Roof, Nice Doorbell 88.46*

SEVEN. STRAIGHT. LOSSES.

No Roof has spent 728 FAAB, traded away half his original roster, and is still finding innovative ways to be terrible. This week he left *22.8 points on the bench* while starting receivers who scored 5.2 and 3.8.

*The man spent an eighth-round pick on a doorbell, threw it away, sold the roof, traded the foundation, and is now sitting in the rubble wondering why the property value keeps falling.*

At this point, the only thing holding the house together is the Sleeper app's refusal to let him trade himself.

*🧿 THE CURSE HAS BEEN TEMPORARILY LIFTED*

*Gibbs me a break 126.18 – Shadow Force 116.18*

After seven weeks of getting repeatedly kicked in the teeth by the fantasy gods, Gibbs finally managed to score 126 points AND win a football game.

Shadow Force actually put up a respectable 116, but apparently forgot that opponents are allowed to have good weeks too.

*Gibbs is now 2–6 despite having nearly 946 points. He's the fantasy equivalent of a billionaire who keeps getting mugged on his way to the bank.*

*📈 THE RETURN OF THE CONTENDER*

*Boutte Call 125.98 – Frank Gore Family Jewels 85.22*

After last week's embarrassing 76-point collapse, Boutte Call bounced back with 126 and absolutely flattened Frank Gore Family Jewels.

Frank Gore managed 85 points, including a zero-point starter and a defense contributing two, despite entering the week on a three-game winning streak.

*Frank Gore spent three weeks convincing everyone he was a contender, then showed up to Week 8 looking like he'd been assembled from the clearance rack at Canadian Tire.*

*🤡 THE BATTLE FOR THE PARTICIPATION TROPHY*

*Saquon my pickens 103.90 – aronv 85.46*

Saquon my pickens secured his third win despite leaving a *33.6-point performance on the bench*, while one of his starters managed just 2.2.

Aronv scored 85, somehow making the lineup incompetence completely irrelevant.

*One manager tried to sabotage his own team and the other was so terrible he couldn't even capitalize on it. This wasn't a matchup. It was two people trying to lose an argument.*

---

## 🏆 WEEK 8 AWARDS

*💀 Biggest Ass-Kicking:* Soy Sheldon — a 95.02-point demolition of Bo Dix.

*👑 Biggest Upset:* For the Love of the Game — ending Michelle's undefeated season with just 101.56 points.

*🤡 Worst Bench Management:* Saquon my pickens — 33.6 points on the bench while winning with 103.9.

*🏚️ Most Condemned Property:* No Roof, Nice Doorbell — seven straight losses and still rearranging the furniture.

*💸 Worst Return on Investment:* Bo Dix — 810 FAAB spent, 79 points scored, and a 95-point loss.

*📉 Biggest Reality Check:* Michelle Poplas Fan Club — from 7–0 juggernaut to 79-point punching bag.

*🧿 Most Cursed Franchise:* Gibbs me a break — finally gets win number two despite being among the league's highest-scoring teams.

*🔥 Biggest Statement:* Soy Sheldon — 174.10 points, three straight wins, and suddenly looking like a genuine contender.

*Final thought:* The league's last undefeated team just lost to a 101-point performance, Soy Sheldon scored 174, and No Roof, Nice Doorbell has now lost seven straight.

*Some managers are building championship teams. Others are building character. No Roof is apparently building neither.*

[5:55 PM] **You:** # 🏈 WEEK 9: THE EMPIRE FALLS, THE DOORBELL RINGS, AND THE TRADE MARKET CLAIMS ANOTHER VICTIM

Nine weeks in, Boutte Call has reclaimed the throne, Michelle's undefeated dynasty has developed a drinking problem, and No Roof, Nice Doorbell has finally won a football game.

Meanwhile, Gibbs me a break is somehow making a late-season comeback, and one manager just paid 100 FAAB to participate in the most embarrassing loss of the week.

## 🔥 THE MATCHUPS

*👑 Boutte Call 136.96 – Saquon my pickens 75.08*

A *61.88-point demolition* that puts Boutte Call at 8–1 and back on top of the league.

Saquon my pickens managed 75 points, including a 5-point running back performance and a 5.6-point starter, while leaving 19.7 points on the bench.

Boutte didn't even need his best lineup. He could've started a lawn chair at flex and still won comfortably.

*The league has a new king, and Saquon was apparently invited to the coronation as the court jester.*

---

*📉 Gibbs me a break 113.66 – Michelle Poplas Fan Club 79.02*

THE 7–0 DYNASTY IS NOW ON A TWO-GAME LOSING STREAK.

Michelle spent 965 FAAB building a supposedly unstoppable roster, only to follow last week's 78.98 with *79.02*.

That's a combined 158 points over two weeks. Soy Sheldon scored 174 in a single game last week.

Meanwhile, Gibbs me a break has won two straight after starting 1–6, including a win against the former undefeated team.

And the timing couldn't be better: Gibbs just completed a trade that brought in two players and *100 FAAB*, while Michelle has practically nothing left to spend.

*One team is experiencing a financial collapse. The other just received a government bailout and immediately used it to overthrow the regime.*

---

*💥 Shadow Force 153.14 – Bo Dix 💩 134.46*

The best offensive shootout of the week, with a combined *287.60 points*.

Shadow Force got 29.54 from his quarterback, plus 28.5 and 27.4 from two other starters. Bo Dix answered with a 35.36-point quarterback and a 34.3-point running back.

And STILL lost by 18.68.

After spending 861 FAAB and suffering through weeks of mediocre scoring, Bo Dix finally assembled a legitimate fantasy performance — only to run directly into the highest-scoring team of the week.

*He spent two months building a tank, finally got it running, and immediately drove it into an aircraft carrier.*

---

*🏚️ No Roof, Nice Doorbell 103.14 – For the Love of the Game 70.66*

THE SEVEN-GAME LOSING STREAK IS OVER.

No Roof finally wins his second game of the season, and naturally it comes against a team that scored *70.66 points*.

But the real comedy is what happened before kickoff.

For the Love of the Game completed a trade with Gibbs me a break, sending away two players and *100 FAAB* in exchange for two others.

Gibbs then beat the former undefeated team.

For the Love of the Game proceeded to lose to the league's most structurally unsound franchise.

*Imagine paying another manager 100 FAAB, watching him overthrow the league's top team, and then becoming the first person in eight weeks to lose to a house without a roof.*

No Roof hasn't repaired the house. The neighbours have simply started collapsing around it.

---

*⚔️ Puka Shell Necklace 125.36 – Frank Gore Family Jewels 121.78*

Finally, an actual competitive football game.

Puka survived by *3.58 points*, thanks in part to a 28-point performance and 25.28 from his quarterback.

Frank Gore had 36.18 from his quarterback and 21.7 from another starter, but also started a player who scored zero while leaving an 18.5-point performance on the bench.

*A potential 18.5-point upgrade in a matchup decided by 3.58.*

After spending the early season winning games with scores in the 90s, Frank Gore finally puts up 122 and loses because his bench apparently had better things to do.

*The fantasy gods have finally sent the invoice for all those discounted wins.*

---

*🥱 Soy Sheldon 95.20 – aronv 78.38*

Soy Sheldon followed last week's 174-point nuclear strike with a 95-point performance and somehow still won.

That's *four consecutive victories*, despite the offense apparently deciding to take the week off.

Aronv managed 78 points while leaving an 18.26-point quarterback performance on the bench instead of the 16.48-point starter. Even that wouldn't have been enough.

*Soy Sheldon went from dropping a hydrogen bomb to throwing a wet napkin, and aronv still couldn't defend himself.*

---

## 🔄 THE TRADE DESK

*Gibbs me a break receives:* Players 13274, 13301 + 100 FAAB.

*For the Love of the Game receives:* Players 11576, 11834.

The trade's long-term winner is still to be determined, but the immediate optics are incredible.

Gibbs wins. For the Love of the Game loses to No Roof.

*This is the fantasy equivalent of selling your car, giving the buyer $100 for gas, and then watching him drive past you while you're waiting for the bus.*

---

## 🏆 WEEK 9 AWARDS

*🥊 Biggest Ass-Kicking:* Boutte Call — 61.88-point demolition of Saquon my pickens.

*💥 Best Matchup:* Shadow Force vs Bo Dix — 287.60 combined points.

*🧿 Unluckiest Loser:* Bo Dix — 134.46 points and still losing by 18.68.

*🤡 Most Costly Lineup Decision:* Frank Gore Family Jewels — an 18.5-point bench performance while starting a zero, losing by just 3.58.

*📉 Biggest Collapse:* Michelle Poplas Fan Club — 7–0 to 7–2, with consecutive 79-point performances.

*💸 Worst Trade Timing:* For the Love of the Game — paid 100 FAAB in a trade, then immediately lost to No Roof.

*🏚️ Municipal Housing Miracle:* No Roof, Nice Doorbell — first win since Week 1, snapping seven straight losses.

*📈 Biggest Comeback:* Gibbs me a break — two straight wins, including knocking off the former undefeated team.

*🍀 Luckiest Winner:* Soy Sheldon — 95.20 points was enough for a fourth consecutive victory.

*👑 New League Favourite:* Boutte Call — 8–1, three straight wins, and the highest total points in the league.

---

## 🏁 FINAL VERDICT

Boutte Call is back on top. Michelle's dynasty is suddenly looking like a Ponzi scheme. Gibbs has discovered winning. No Roof has discovered electricity.

And For the Love of the Game just paid 100 FAAB to help another team beat the league's former powerhouse while getting embarrassed by the league's favourite construction disaster.

*Week 9 proved that money can't buy happiness, but apparently it can buy someone else's happiness while you lose to a guy who drafted a doorbell before installing a roof.*

[12:41 PM] **You:** # 🏈 WEEK 10: THE PLAYOFF PANIC BUTTON HAS BEEN PRESSED

Ten weeks in, Michelle has remembered how to play football, the playoff bubble is turning into a knife fight, and No Roof, Nice Doorbell is somehow the hottest team in the bottom half of the standings.

Meanwhile, one manager scored 69 points in a must-win matchup, another left 35 points at quarterback on the bench, and Frank Gore Family Jewels continues proving that you don't actually need to be good at fantasy football to win.

## 🔥 THE MATCHUPS

*👑 Jaggin’ Goff 99.32 – aronv 60.92*

Jaggin’ Goff improves to *9–1*, extending the winning streak to four games without even cracking 100 points.

Aronv delivered the week's lowest score, but the real achievement was starting a quarterback who scored *3.02 points while leaving a 35.42-point performance on the bench*.

That's 32.4 points of potential improvement at one position. Still not enough to win, but at least the defeat could've looked like an actual football game.

*Jaggin’ Goff showed up with a butter knife. Aronv brought a spoon, dropped it, and stabbed himself in the foot.*

---

*💥 Golden’s Girls 138.16 – Soy Sheldon 101.62*

The four-game Soy Sheldon winning streak is DEAD.

Golden’s Girls dropped 138 points, led by a 33.16-point quarterback and another 24.3-point performance, to improve to 6–4.

Soy Sheldon had two starters combine for *2.5 points*, and a recently acquired quarterback scored 15.38 on the bench while the starter scored 34.42. At least the QB decision was correct.

*Soy Sheldon spent four weeks convincing everyone he was a championship contender, then showed up to the biggest part of the season with half his roster apparently working from home.*

Golden’s Girls is now one of the most dangerous teams in the playoff race.

---

*⚔️ Puka Shell Necklace 141.86 – Shough and Awe 132.98*

The best matchup of the week.

Puka scored 141.86, with seven starters reaching double digits, and survived Shough and Awe's 132.98-point performance by just *8.88 points*.

Shough and Awe had a 38-point explosion and a 23.2-point performance, but his quarterback scored only 9.98 while a 26.78-point alternative sat on the bench.

*That's a 16.8-point QB difference in a matchup decided by 8.88.*

A potential win was sitting on the bench while Shough and Awe watched his playoff hopes take another beating.

*Puka is 7–3. Shough and Awe is 3–7. One is preparing for the playoffs, and the other is preparing an appeal to the fantasy gods.*

---

*📈 Michelle Poplas Fan Club 152.06 – Gav's Not So Groovy Team 95.84*

THE RECESSION IS OVER.

After consecutive 79-point disasters, Michelle responded with *152.06*, the highest score of Week 10.

Four starters scored at least 20, including a 31.7-point performance, and Minnesota's defense chipped in another 20.

Gav's Not So Groovy Team, meanwhile, scored 95.84 despite starting a player who contributed 0.3 points.

*Michelle's $965 FAAB investment has recovered. Unfortunately for Gav, the economic stimulus arrived in the form of a 56-point beating.*

The former undefeated team is now 8–2 and back in the championship conversation.

---

*🏚️ No Roof, Nice Doorbell 110.90 – I Gibbs zero Bucks 98.96*

THE DOORBELL IS ON A WINNING STREAK.

After losing seven straight, No Roof has now won TWO consecutive games.

A 32.1-point performance and another 21.7 carried the team past I Gibbs zero Bucks, whose 40.7-point explosion from one starter was almost half his entire offensive production.

I Gibbs zero Bucks had five other starters combine for just *16.1 points*.

*Imagine getting 40 points from one player and still losing to a building that was declared uninhabitable six weeks ago.*

No Roof is now 3–7. The house still has no roof, but apparently the electrical system has been restored.

---

*🪦 Frank Gore Family Jewels 81.90 – For the Love of the Game 69.24*

THE WEEK'S MOST OFFENSIVE FOOTBALL GAME.

Not offensive as in high-scoring. Offensive as in a crime against the sport.

Frank Gore won with *81.90 points*, despite starting a player who scored zero and getting 44.3 points from a single player — more than half his entire team total.

For the Love of the Game somehow managed just *69.24 points*, losing to a team whose other eight starters combined for 37.6.

After last week's 70.66-point embarrassment against No Roof, For the Love of the Game has now scored *139.90 points across two weeks*.

*Michelle scored 152 this week alone.*

For the Love of the Game has gone from playoff contender to a team that could lose to a spreadsheet with a random-number generator.

*Frank Gore didn't win this matchup. He simply remained conscious longer than his opponent.*

---

## 🔄 THE TRANSACTION DESK

*💰 The 100-FAAB Trade Carousel*

Golden’s Girls and For the Love of the Game completed a trade:

- *Golden’s Girls receives:* Player 2133
- *For the Love of the Game receives:* Player 12481 and 100 FAAB

That's another 100 FAAB changing hands involving For the Love of the Game, immediately after the previous week's trade with I Gibbs zero Bucks.

Golden’s Girls wins its matchup. For the Love of the Game loses with 69 points.

*For the Love of the Game has apparently stopped managing a fantasy football team and started operating a currency exchange.*

*🔄 The Waiver-Wire Revolving Door*

Gav's Not So Groovy Team added player 11199, replaced him with 12969, then replaced that player with 11577.

Three acquisitions, one week, and a 56-point loss.

*At this point, the roster has a higher employee turnover rate than a fast-food restaurant during a health inspection.*

---

## 🏆 THE PLAYOFF RACE

Assuming six playoff spots.

*🔒 The leaders*

*Jaggin’ Goff (9–1)* — Four straight wins and the clear leader. First-round bye territory.

*Michelle Poplas Fan Club (8–2)* — The 152-point rebound suggests the two-week collapse may have been temporary.

*Puka Shell Necklace (7–3)* — Three straight wins and looking increasingly secure.

*⚔️ The three-way knife fight*

*Golden’s Girls (6–4)* — Two straight wins and 138 points this week. Momentum is building.

*Soy Sheldon (6–4)* — Four-game winning streak snapped. Suddenly the cushion doesn't look so comfortable.

*Frank Gore Family Jewels (6–4)* — Just won with 81.9 points. The record looks great; the weekly scoring is increasingly concerning.

*🚨 First team outside the projected playoff line*

*For the Love of the Game (5–5)* — Two straight losses, scoring 70.66 and 69.24. The playoffs are slipping away while the team is apparently busy negotiating trades.

*🪦 The desperate outsiders*

*I Gibbs zero Bucks (3–7)* — Still among the league's highest-scoring teams, but running out of time to turn points into wins.

*Shough and Awe (3–7)* — Scored 133 and lost. A devastating result for a team that desperately needed the win.

*No Roof, Nice Doorbell (3–7)* — Two straight wins. The greatest comeback story in municipal housing history is technically still alive.

*Gav's Not So Groovy Team (2–8)* — Four straight losses. The playoff dream is on life support.

*aronv (2–8)* — Three straight losses and a 60.92-point performance. Someone should check whether this team is still connected to the internet.

---

## 🏆 WEEK 10 AWARDS

*🥊 Biggest Ass-Kicking:* Michelle Poplas Fan Club — 56.22-point demolition of Gav's Not So Groovy Team.

*🤡 Worst QB Decision:* aronv — 35.42 points on the bench instead of 3.02 in the starting lineup.

*💔 Most Painful Potential Start/Sit:* Shough and Awe — 26.78 QB points benched in an 8.88-point loss.

*💀 Most Embarrassing Matchup:* Frank Gore Family Jewels vs For the Love of the Game — 151.14 combined points. An insult to competitive athletics.

*🍀 Luckiest Winner:* Frank Gore Family Jewels — 81.90 points was somehow enough.

*📉 Biggest Playoff Collapse:* For the Love of the Game — consecutive scores of 70.66 and 69.24 while falling to 5–5.

*🔥 Biggest Statement:* Michelle Poplas Fan Club — 152.06 points after two straight losses.

*🏚️ Most Unexpected Winning Streak:* No Roof, Nice Doorbell — two consecutive wins after losing seven straight.

*💸 Most Active Currency Exchange:* For the Love of the Game — another trade involving 100 FAAB.

*👑 Team to Beat:* Jaggin’ Goff — 9–1, four straight wins, and the best record in the league.

---

## 🏁 FINAL VERDICT

The top three are separating themselves, three teams are fighting for the remaining projected playoff spots, and For the Love of the Game is attempting to trade its way out of a crisis that apparently requires basic offensive production.

Michelle is back. Golden’s Girls is surging. Shough and Awe is running out of time. I Gibbs zero Bucks is running out of miracles.

And No Roof, Nice Doorbell has won two straight.

*At this rate, the house might actually get a roof before For the Love of the Game scores 100 points again.*
